ParlaMint-AT_2008-07-10-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00068_d7e3137	Mrs Bundesminister, please.

ParlaMint-AT_2008-07-10-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00068_d7e3246	Victim protection is indeed a determining topic of my work. We have been very much helped by the innovations that took place with the entry into force of the criminal process reform. The new criminal code provides for a very, very extended protection of the victims and much more information and control rights of the victims. It is gratifying to see that the victims also make strong use of these new opportunities. We have very, very many requests for continuation, requests against the initiation of proceedings by the public prosecutor, because the victims want the procedure to take place or want good reasons why it is not necessary to continue the procedure. We have expanded the process support, and in particular we now also provide for the possibility of process support and other instruments of victim protection for the civil process.

It called for a referendum on our accession to the European Union on June 1994. Two thirds of the Austrian population voted for accession at that time. That was a very unexpected result at the time. I remember being addressed a year before that – I was a young Member – by well-known colleagues who asked: Why are you so committed to Austria's accession to the European Union? That's a hopeless thing. We will never win this referendum in the scepticism that exists in Austria on this issue! I can remember that at this time the agreement to join is below 30 percent. It is in a very big discussion in Austria, in which many of them – especially the social partners, all those political forces who thought it was right, many mayors –, in many critical discussions where yes and no, Pro and Contra were weighed, managed to convince a large majority of the Austrian population, and then two thirds of the population voted for it completely unexpectedly. I say: It was a real one, it was a groundbreaking and very important decision for the future of Austria, which I am standing on today. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] If you look at this in retrospect: Did the Austrians really decide, even from their own (if you want to) self-interested perspective?, and: How has Austria developed since accession?, then – I think – there are some very interesting data. In the period since we are a member of the European Union, Austrian exports have more than tripled. That is to say, the Austrian economy has become a lot, a lot of international, while it was still in 1994. We have succeeded in making progress towards the fourth place in the Member States of the European Union in terms of our gross domestic product per capita, i.e. the entire wealth of our country. In front of us are Luxembourg, Ireland and only the Netherlands, behind that already comes Austria. At this time, we have succeeded in dramatically reducing unemployment, especially in the last two years, and in unemployment we are one of the best countries in Europe at 4.1 percent in the meantime. The Austrian economy has developed above average at this time, because our growth rates are above the average of the euro zone, i.e. among the better developed countries of the European Union, and we are able to keep this growth edge even in this year. To this end, we have to say: This overall success development of Austria in the European Union was very often accompanied by fears. You have to admit that quite openly. ParlaMint-AT_2008-07-10-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00068_d7e9219	Mr President. Appreciate members of the government! High house! A social Europe is almost reflective in every EU discussion. But what should this social Europe look like? Should Member States hand over their competences in the social field to Europe? Should there be only a European and no national social policy in the future? Do European citizens even want a European social policy that sets uniform standards for all 27 Member States? Do Austrian citizens want the same social level as in Portugal, Poland, the Czech Republic, Romania or Bulgaria? Should this be this much-priced social Europe? Is it not preferable for the Member States to remain with them in the field of social policy and for the EU to become only supportive and complementary? [[Abg. Hornek: Yes!]] If that is what it means by a social Europe, then I can calm you all because we already have it. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] [[Abg. Strache: The opposite is the case!]] The problem is that most people who demand a social Europe do not know what Europe is in this area. I am opposed to calling for a social Europe and simply to hide what has already been achieved in the social field at national level. It is true, of course, that the EC has started as an economic community. At the outset, social policy measures were only adopted if this was appropriate for the creation and development of the internal market. However, this changed in 1987 with the entry into force of the Single European Act. As a result, the Maastricht, Amsterdam and Nice Treaties have continuously expanded European social skills. Even in those areas of social policy in which the EU has little or no competence – because there are major differences in political interests, social standards, values and preferences – the Union is not inactive. In this context, the European Union is trying, by means of the instrument of the Open Method of Coordination, to establish the path between a stronger integration of Europe on the one hand and the remaining political sovereignty among the national states on the other. All of this should be known to those who constantly demand a social Europe. But do you even know how far Europe's social protection actually goes? The concept of social protection covers not only social welfare, but also employment. Do you know, for example, that the European Union has responded to the decision of a large international group to carry out the planned job reduction in the Member State in which the terminations are most rapid and lightest with the adoption of the so-called mass dismissal directive? This directive provides for the protection of workers in the event of mass redundancies. Do you know that the Union has opted for protection against social dumping in the fight between unlimited freedom to provide services and protection against social dumping? [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] What does that mean? – This means that, for example, in the case of temporary posting of an EU-foreign worker to Austria, the hard core of Austrian labour law applies to these workers. In other words, these posted workers are equal to Austrian workers. The Union therefore intends to exclude the risk of abuse and exploitation of posted workers. Do you know that this draft directive, which I have now referred to, has preceded a number of decisions of the European Court of Justice, for example the Rush Portuguesa decision? Do you know that it is very often the European Court of Justice that is driving forward social policy developments? For example, do you know the decisions Christel Schmidt or the abler decision, by the way an Austrian case, or the case of Mahlburg, the case of Kalanke, Jäger, Simap – and so on, I could mention many more –? Do you know the many directives on the protection of workers: the Part-Time Labour Directive, the Directive on temporary employment, the Mother Protection Directive, the Equal Treatment Directive, the Transition of Workers Directive, the Proof Directive? It is a pity, because if you knew the relevant jurisdiction, directives and regulations, you would know what Europe's social character is in today and would be much more credible in your demand for a social Europe. [[President Dr. Spindelegger gives the bell sign.]] Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2008-07-10-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00068_d7e11542	We're now on the 2nd. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. The first speaker to speak is Dr. Fichtenbauer, Member of Parliament, with a speaking time of 4 minutes. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2008-07-10-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00068_d7e11569	Madam President. If anyone on the part of the government should be there, of course I greet him warmly, without wanting to drive you to an excessive hurry! [[State Secretary Dr. Matznetter: (taking place on the government bank) Sorry!]] [[Secretary of State Dr. Matznetter: I want to listen to you too!]] – That’s very sweet of you! Thank you. The Law of Procuratorship thus floats in the shadow of the great political things, but from my point of view is to be viewed in a specific way. Based on the Procuratorial Act or this Novelle or the new Legal Content, a mass-ve Inconsistency of the principle political statements that we have heard in recent years is to be established. First point: It should be acknowledged that in particular the form of the President of the Financial Procuratorship as a driving force, who has endeavoured to improve the legal status, is nothing against and this effort is honorable and worthy of recognition. Secondly, However, it must be noted that the compaction and the proliferation of legal acts, which are otherwise reserved for the professions in their activity, in concentration and strengthening of this activity in a structure which is to be attributed to Austrian offices, is at least contre coeur of the ÖVP philosophy of the last ten years. In the light of a development, that for everything that is not necessarily to be asked by the state, disseminations, private-sector subjects have been established in order not to allow these tasks to be solved by the State, a specific state organization in its functionality is increasingly re-structured by competences, in some way, and their activity is, so to speak, bound up. This situation requires a critical attitude towards this project and is also to be described as extremely critical in some detail. If, and in so far as a lawyer's activity is carried out for a client, two elements of the case are indispensable: Number one is the silence; and number two, to follow exclusively the client interests and to be active in the sense of client interests. Even from the law and some content that have been sufficiently illuminated in advance and during the discussion, the lack of ability to implement these principles is obvious. An office subordinated to and incorporated into the Ministry of Finance may not comply with the services to be provided by the freely chosen lawyers of the confidentiality and full independence of other public interests and the exclusive service of the client's well-being. In addition, collision situations, i.e. consideration of whether, in the case of representativeness of a state structure against the other, there is a potential situation where conflicting interests are at the disposal of each other, the procuratorate can choose which of the two public authorities, whether federal or local, to represent them, are unthinkable in a process relating to the law. In such a case, no lawyer should take over one of the two mandates. For this reason, because the basic structure of the sub-intelligent activity, to operate in a way that is practically functional, cannot be voted, cannot be done, because an office can never replace a freely elected lawyer, we will reject this law. However, if it is decided today, I promise for the political future that we will not forget this matter, but will bring it into play recently. ParlaMint-AT_2008-07-10-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00068_d7e13530	Madam President. Mr State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! The 2008 Tax Change Act amends the Basic Tax Act or adjustments to the Alcohol Tax Act, the Beer Tax Act, the Sparkling Tax Act, the Tobacco Tax Act and the Mineral Oil Tax Act. The legal exemption rules provide for the equality of international organisations with embassies at bilateral level for land ownership, which is used by an international organisation as permanent headquarters. The head office of an international organisation is an area which is, in principle, inviolable. Therefore, the basic tax exemption is fair and easy to understand when it is a permanent residence. This will certainly make it more interesting for international organisations to establish a permanent location in Austria. Forcing eGovernment in excise duties is the main reason for the change in the law, with priority being given to the service concept of financial management vis-à-vis economic operators. This will make it possible for applications, applications and refunds to no longer be filed in paper form, but electronically via FinanzOnline. This amendment is timely and leads to easier and practical processing and to a reduction of the administrative burden on the part of the taxable person and the tax authority. Administrative simplification makes it possible to carry out an ongoing plausibility check, a statistical evaluation of the consumption tax data automatically and on demand. The main focus of this law is on simplifying and reducing the administrative burden on citizens, as well as on tax authorities. So this is another step towards reducing bureaucracy.

Ladies and gentlemen! ==References== The deadline for the implementation of the Third Money laundering Directive expired on December 2007. This third money laundering directive has almost been fully transposed into Austrian law; it is only the implementation of the casinos directive. The European Commission has therefore already initiated infringement proceedings against Austria. In order to highlight the importance of this Directive, the European Commission also pointed out that these money laundering directives are for the protection of the financial system, the functioning of the internal market and public order in the European Union. Austria is a member of FATF, Financial Action Task Force on Money Lauthering. In this body, experts have the task of analysing the methods of money laundering and developing measures to combat money laundering and terrorist financing. In September 2008, Austria will be examined by the International Monetary Fund for compliance with these FATF recommendations on combating money laundering and terrorist financing. These provisions of the Directive must be implemented in order to ensure a positive assessment and thus the preservation of the reputation of Austria. Money laundering usually has the purpose of concealing the origin of money, hiding the money from access to law enforcement and financial services, and transferring profits from the shadow economy to the legal area. The novelle of the gambling law requires the visitors of a playbench that they are of age and prove their identity with a valid official photo identification. This data shall be kept for at least five years by the central bank operator. With this measure of amendment of the gambling law Austria now meets the international standard.

ParlaMint-AT_2008-07-10-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00068_d7e14485	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! High house! The whole subject of the right of visit is an important area, which deserves more attention even in the context of family policy as a whole. The number of cases relating to the right to visit is increasing steadily, with around 5 300 in 2007. This shows us, ladies and gentlemen, that living together in the family – that is, parents and children in the common household – often does not work anymore. There are certainly many reasons for this, and it is not always easy as a parent always to do the right thing at the right time for the benefit of the children and the family. For me, this is actually the point in this debate: It is about the child's well-being, ladies and gentlemen. As a double family father, I know that there are often different views between father and mother in working families when it comes to what is good for the child and what is not. One should also set quality before quantity: It is not about the number of hours spent with the child, but about how to spend time with the children. You should not create any rivals between your mother and your father if a parent promises everything to the child and gives everything and the other parent has to hold it back. And if the parents do not live in the common household, the differences are often even greater. Partnership is one, being a parent is the other. It must be clear to parents, whether in common or in separate households, that it is about the child's well-being, and each child has a right to mother and father in principle. There are, of course, exceptions in divorces or disputes if, for example, the child is in danger of delay or is threatened with sexual abuse. This has already been mentioned. Then it is clear that there can be no such right. On the other hand, on the other hand, it cannot be that the mother or the father determines, for personal reasons, against the other parent, what is good for the child and what is not, whether the father or grandparents may see the children or not. The problem often lies already in the divorce itself. I would like to address this issue here, because the whole problem of the right of visit is actually only to be dealt with. So there is often the cause already there, at the end, at the end of the separation or whatever. Often the separation is also due to small things. Anyone who lives in a community of life who is married knows that one can have different views that there is also a dispute, but unfortunately this often goes so far as to say that one can no longer say to each other, one no longer wants to have one another, it is only disputed and one goes apart. And the kids actually stay on the track. It is therefore important to start there and start a mediation – a kind of assistance, as the Minister has announced – and to try to establish a basis between the parents so that the partnership can continue together. This would be necessary and useful from my point of view, because you can also talk things out, and you also have to give up and, above all, you can forgive. Injuries that the parents have committed to each other must be able to be processed. From my point of view, the steps must be taken so that in many cases it does not even come so far as to clarify the question of the right of visit. If it comes to this, Mrs Federal Minister – I am already referring to the question – that the question of the right of visit must be clarified, then it must be examined and raised in detail, because if there are any accusations, it must be clarified whether they are entitled or not and whether they can come to the right of visit or not. Personal sensibilities between parents have actually lost little. And, finally, I would like to stress that the great title under which we are approaching this area must read: The focus is on the child! Thank you.

These are essentially already parts of a government template that lies in the Committee. On the one hand, this is about treating citizens of the European Economic Area or the European Union with regard to the rules on player protection as well as nationals. That means that if they play a lot, they have to show their ID. If they play a lot, play often and lose a lot, they have to prove that they can afford this – in connection with consultations et cetera. So this is equality. The second is the ban on advertising. This means that play banks located abroad may also advertise in Austria for the visit. That's what's been up to now. However, the European Union believes that this is Community-wide. This will be possible now, but with the limitation that this advertising possibility must be requested in the Ministry of Finance, and two things will be required: Firstly, it is assumed that the concession award complies with European directives, and secondly, that the player protection rules in each country have at least the standard as in Austria – otherwise they must not advertise. So you can't advertise by saying: Aha, you're locked up at the casino in Austria, then you'll come across the border and play with us! – This is not possible because the security or player protection mechanisms must be the same. New is also another penalty. This refers to banks and the like, who directly participate, i.e. have a contractual connection with gambling banks that have their headquarters abroad, have no concession, but offer games over the Internet, for example, in Austria. If banks are directly involved here, there are criminal provisions here that are clearly forbidden – because it is forbidden to offer games from abroad without concession in Austria.

ParlaMint-AT_2008-07-10-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00068_d7e15672	We're on the 5th. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. We are therefore going into the debate. The first speaker was Mr Dr. Zinggl. 4 minutes of voluntary time limit. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2008-07-10-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00068_d7e15703	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! This bill concerns liability for a single exhibition in a 500 million € museum. It is a classic event legislation in the negative sense with probably wild effects on museum policy. I tried to explain this in the Financial Committee, but the chairman stupidly – yes, what did he do? – has said that I should deal with the Committee on Culture. [[Abg. Dr. stupid: After what did he do?]] That's what I did. The Committee on Culture, on the other hand, has said that it belongs to the Financial Committee – where I was before. In one word: No one was responsible for it. Now the plenary is concerned with this, and I would like to explain my criticism. [[Abg. Dr. Stummvoll: In the financial committee it was already!]] - Yeah, we talked about it, but you know what was going on anyway. I don't need to remind you. [[Abg. Morak: But that would interest us!]] I can explain it to you later in a private vote, Mr Morak, or next time you come to the Financial Committee or to the Committee on Culture, you have not been there for a long time, in case you do not know. What is this law about? What is it about the federal liability at all? – The original sense of federal liability was to cover your own property – “cover” cannot be said, for it is precisely not possible to give up insurance. They say: Okay, if anything's disappeared or destroyed, it's just gone. This is similar to a family that has a garden house and the garden house is not insured. If it burns down, it's just gone, you can't do anything. That's all right with works of art. But in 2006, the museum directors, first and foremost director Seipel, came up with the idea that there are exhibitions for which images, works are borrowed from the outside – and who is liable for them? This liability would have to be financed by the basic fee. It was then tried to assert a federal liability. We were against it at the time, but it went on. In other words, since 2006, there has been 1 billion €, which are held together for all federal museums as liability for borrowed works. That means if such a work disappears now, then we pay for it, then we pay more or less cash for it. [[Abg. Dr. Graf: That's what it used to be!]] - Well, not bar. Of course, we pay exactly. [[Abg. Dr. Graf: Whether the Federal Museum pays or – that's the same!]] - No, that's not true. The Federal Museum would have to insure it privately – Mr. Col., I will explain it to you again in detail – and then it would take over the insurance. That's exactly what it's all about. The museum saves itself this acquisition by the insurance and thus the insurance premium, and now we are all jointly liable for it. That would be so when compared to the family that it rents a garden house somewhere, this burns down, and we have to replace it! That's something else. Fortunately, nothing has happened since 2006. Now, however, a very large exhibition is coming in the Albertina of Director Schröder, which is not covered with the whole billion, and now we are, as it were, liable for this additional 500 million; the proposal for a law goes in this direction. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Morak.]] Also again with the explanatory statement in the cover: So far, nothing has happened. – This statement reminds me very strongly of an argument: For two years there has been no nuclear power station around Austria and has not been an accident, so we can build one in peace! A little bit, Mr Morak, will be reminded of Schiller’s ballad “The Diver” and learn nothing from it. This is a disaster anyway, because the whole blockbuster carousel is properly heated and because now other museums can automatically come and say: We want a huge Andy Warhol exhibition, we want a Leonardo exhibition, we want a Old Timer exhibition! – And in any case, the Federation is liable. That is, if nothing happens, is a museum director, as in this case Klaus Albrecht Schröder, the hero, he has the full success, but if something happens, we pay full. In fact, it is naïve to say: There is nothing going to happen, and nothing has happened so far, because if something happens, up to 500 million will be beating, and that is the five times the basic fee per year that we spend as a whole. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Kainz.]] I'm telling you something, just about the ÖVP, I can say something about it: This is also a distortion of competition vis-à-vis all private exhibition providers. [[Abg. Kainz: If we didn't do it, you would be against it too!]] A second story in this context is the argument: We want to make great exhibitions, don't beautify you! – Yes, of course, as a high-risk act you can make everything possible, but I think you should, if you want to do something already, so that more people go to the museums, force free entry. In this respect, the SPÖ has promised a lot, there is a declaration of government, but nothing has been done. Thank you.

This is a very important principle for us, because it cannot be that human rights affect or hinder another person. I believe that the time is a more favourable one, because obviously China is looking for public recognition and believes that it can also be achieved by performing the Olympic Games, among other things. In the marriage agreements, two persons of different sex legally declare their will to live in inseparable communion, to bear children, to raise them, and to assist each other.” We liberals believe that these paragraphs should be extended by a single sentence, namely: Marriage and family are under the special protection of state order!, and these paragraphs should be placed in the constitutional rank. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] In view of the fact that there is now a clear need to open up equal-sex partnerships to the possibility of a similar legalisation of their cohabitation, it is necessary to ensure constitutional protection of the terms ‘married’ and ‘family’. Existing discrimination in this area should obviously be merely claimed. I now come to two very important expert opinions. Ms. University Professor Dr. Constanze Fischer-Czermak has carried out the following in a very significant, long article in the April issue of the Notary Newspaper: ‘Neither the Constitutional Court considers that, after the caselaw of the European Court of Human Rights, a State is obliged to allow for a marriage among the same sexes.’ For us liberals, public interest in this equality with pre- and family circumstances is not recognizable. It is therefore not a matter of family equality, but rather of a personal privilege of co-existence with other non-marital partners. Not least, the introduction of a “Ehe light” also constitutes a evasion of the previous marriage. In turn, I quote Mrs. Dr. Constanze Fischer-Czermak: ‘The registered life partnership is intended only for same-sex couples. Thus, marriage is not to get a competition through a ‘Ehe light’ and’ – which appears to be consensus – “in addition, persons of different sexes are reserved.” However, these requirements – and now the point, ladies and gentlemen – lead to a legal dilemma. In fact, ladies and gentlemen, this “Wealth Light” is a privileged partnership – privileged to a different sexual non-marital partnership. If one went to the Constitutional Court, then the “Ehe light” would also be used for various sexual partnerships in Bälde and Gäbe. Ladies and gentlemen, I would have another gentleman whom I would like to quote – but I have already seen that this will not happen over time. This is Dr. Jakob Cornides, Board of Directors of the European Commission, a ten-page article in the legal papers of May 2008. He'll end up at the same time. We liberals believe that this discrimination, the “Ehe light”, should not apply to normal different sex partnerships. That is why we believe: The marriage, as defined in the Austrian ABGB, is protected and therefore in the constitutional rank.

There's something we can do for families, there's a need for action. 20 percent of all women, it is said, experience violence by a close male family at least once. There's a need for action. Any abstract, ideologically motivated constitutional provisions have lost nothing in today's reality. That's why we're going another way. We try to meet people so that they can shape their lives the way they want them to. We offer them solutions and do not hold any teaching formulas high for any ideological reason. Thank you.
ParlaMint-AT_2008-07-10-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00068_d7e20125	The next speaker is Mr Magistrate. Darmann. 2 minutes desired speaking time. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2008-07-10-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00068_d7e20146	Madam President. (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) At this first reading, I should like to address this request from the FPÖ in a few points. On the one hand, I should like to say that politics is just as much called for to address the existing social realities and to reflect them accordingly in the legislative text, and not – and this must not now, please, be understood incorrectly – to replicate, under quotations, “overcome” forms of society and even to lift them into the constitutional rank; “overcome” in the sense that family law and parts of the ABGB, Mr. Graf, are really in need of reform. This is not only seen in the spelling – the year of origin has already been mentioned – but also because this section, which you now want to raise in the constitutional order, provides that a family exists only when children are born. The fact, however, that social fact is that there are patchwork families these days, a combination of people who also create a family, because there are children there too. That is, “breeding, ” as in this paragraph, should not be exclusively the case. Moreover, it is of course also important for us to work against a shattering of the Constitution and, on the contrary, to achieve a constitutional readjustment and not to bring more and more regulations into the constitutional ranks. We have only seen today, we have seen in recent months what has not been secured in the Constitution. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Dr. Graf.]] Finally, Mr Graf, last point, allow me to make it clear that, of course, in our opinion, the family and only the children must be at the heart of the work of politics. - Thank you.
